The clip sits where the input and the repeats meet in the tank: Soft is linear to 0.8 x the level then a tanh knee to the level, Hard clips at the level, Limit is a stereo-linked limiter (instant attack, 50 ms release). The old fixed soft limit (linear to 1, knee to 1.8) is gone; the default is Soft at 0 dB. The page is laid out in sections like the old Character page, with faint `live.line` hairlines between them (`OPT_SEPS`): Feedback Clip (mode, Level) beside the curve; Ducking (square on/off toggle beside its title, Threshold, Release) beside Divisions; Spectrum Dry / Wet beside Link Latency; then a full-width row with Repitch (square toggle) and its `Smoothing Time` (`repsmooth`, 10..2000 ms, default 120 ms = the old fixed glide: the one-pole time constant of the read head's glide to a new time). Ducking and Repitch sit here while the Character page is off (same parameters). Audio cases `repitch_fast` / `repitch_default` / `repitch_slow` check the tap time 100 ms after a 100 -> 300 ms jump against `1 - exp(-100 / smoothing)`.
  - **Divisions** (`Triplet` / `Dotted` toggles, parameters `divtrip` / `divdot`, UI state, both on by default): whether the Division dials offer triplet / dotted values. The enum stays the full 27 values (so automation and saved values keep their meaning); the face skips the switched-off ones: a value that is not offered moves on to the next offered value in the direction the dial turned (`dpDivSnap`), and the dial is corrected with `set` through the Stereo Link mirror route (no output, no loop). Switching an option off moves a side sitting on such a value to the nearest offered length.
  - **Clip LED** (`obj-clipled`, `src/delayplus_clipled.js`, 8 px, right after the page tabs, visible on every page): lights while the Feedback Clip reduces the loop. It is a fixed red (`LED_RED`), not a theme colour, because some themes map Live's alert / selection colours to yellow. The Start gen writes scope slot 28 (`DP_SCOPE.CLIP_DB`) = the deepest clip out / in ratio in dB over the last 32 windows of 64 samples (about 45 ms, so a 33 ms poll never misses a transient; not while frozen). The LED reads it every 33 ms and lights below -0.1 dB, held about 1 s and then fading (`dpClipStep`, `DP_CLIP_HOLD` / `DP_CLIP_FADE`). In v8, `Buffer.peek` of one sample returns a plain number, not an array.
  - **Modulation**: the LFO display (`obj-lfodisp`, `src/delayplus_lfo.js`: L blue / R orange waveforms, live dots from the scope telemetry, clickable waveform icons -> `Mod Waveform`), then `Sync` / `Rate` (Hz or note value) / `Phase`, `x4` and `Mod Delay`, `Mod Filter`, `Env Mix`.
  - **Character** (hidden for now, `SHOW_CHARACTER`): `Gate`, `Ducking`, `Noise`, `Wobble` sections (on toggle + two values each) and `Repitch`.
- **Right column**: `Freeze`, `Diffusion` (dial) with its `Diff Size` bar (reads `Size 60 %`; while a side is in MIDI mode the MIDI From menus take the top of this slot, so the dial moves down under them and the bar hides), `Stereo` (width), `Output`, the `Stereo` / `Ping Pong` / `Mid/Side` stack, `Dry/Wet`.
- **Show / hide** (Echo's mechanisms): one `pak page filterDisplay modSync` feeds an `expr` per widget group -> `prepend hidden` (plus the ring's size). All three inputs are stored parameters, so the state comes back on load. Live's UI font has no triangle / link glyphs, so those two toggles are tiny icon v8uis (`src/delayplus_icon.js`, `DP_ICON` set per instance by the build) driving `live.text` parameters that stay out of the presentation.
- **Stereo Link** is done in the face script: a change of one side's Time Mode, time, note value, Sync Mode, note, fine tune or multiplier is sent to the other side's control with `set` (outlet 1 `mirror <key> <v>` -> `route` -> `prepend set`), which does not output, so it cannot loop. Because `set` does not output, a mirrored Time Mode is also routed into that side's widget swap hub. Switching Link on copies Left onto Right, but only after `init_link` (`dpLoaded`), so loading a Set never rewrites saved values.
- **Times / LFO rate** are computed in the face (`dpSideMs`, `dpLfoHz` in `src/delayplus_config.js`) and poked into the gen header (`hdr[31]` left / mid ms, `hdr[28]` right / side ms, `hdr[27]` LFO Hz).
- `scripts/build_delayplus.py` holds the parameter table `P` (Echo-style long names); a layout module must present every key exactly once and may declare extra displays (`v8uis`: script, rect, the keys whose `set_<key>` they receive, telemetry `names`, `icon`).

- **Theme colours** (follow Live's theme and hue, like Echo): patcher objects bind colour attributes with `"saved_attribute_attributes": {"<attr>": {"expression": "themecolor.live_..."}}` next to a static fallback (`theme()` helper in `scripts/build_delayplus.py`). A `panel` must bind **`bgfillcolor`** (what it paints with; Ableton's own patches do this), binding `bgcolor` changes nothing. v8ui scripts read `max.getcolor("live_...")` via `dpTheme(name, fallback)` on every paint and `dpThemeWatch()` redraws once a second if the theme changed. An LCD-style `live.numbox` (`appearance 4`) draws its value in **`lcdcolor`** in Live, not `textcolor`, so bind both. Hairline accents use `live.line` (its colour defaults to the theme's `live_surface_frame`); a 1 px `panel` drew 3 px thick. Among `background` panels Max draws earlier boxes over later ones, so separators come first, then the section panels, then the body (`live_desktop`). Names used: `live_lcd_bg` (displays, selected tab), `live_surface_bg` (side sections), `live_lcd_title` / `live_lcd_control_fg` (display labels / values), `live_display_line_one/two`, `live_display_handle_one/two`, `live_display_scale_text`, `live_lcd_frame`, `live_control_bg/fg`, `live_control_selection`, `live_alert`.
- **LOM values of exponent parameters are the linear position**, not the shown value: `L Mult` (0.02..50, exponent 5.67) shows x1.00 at LOM `value` 25.0, and writing 1.5 shows x0.02 (`str_for_value` maps it the same way). Tests that set such parameters through the bridge must invert the exponent: `lom = min + (max - min) * ((v - min) / (max - min)) ^ (1 / exponent)`.
- **Parameter names in Live are the short names** (`parameter_shortname`): the LOM `name` / `original_name` and Live's automation and mapping lists use them (verified in Live 12.4.6), so every short name in `P` must be unique (`L Division`, `Gate Thr`, `Duck Rel`, ...). Widgets whose face label differs (the Left / Right time dials hold two parameters each) set `showname 0` and get a `live.comment` label.

### DSP additions (Echo controls)
- Start: per-side times, Mid/Side (the tank runs in M/S; taps are converted back to L/R before the chain, the return to M/S before the tank), Ping Pong, Input gain + Clip Dry + Gate on the signal entering the tank only, Feedback Invert, filter on/off with resonance (`k = 1.4142 * (1 - 0.95 * res)`) and LFO filter modulation (+-2 octaves), LFO (6 shapes, right-channel phase, x4, blended with an input envelope by `Env Mix`) on the delay time, Wobble (random wow / flutter, morph), Noise in the loop (hiss -> crackle), Repitch (tape glide vs crossfade).
- **Diffusion** (Start): four Schroeder allpasses per tank channel on the feedback return (lengths 113 / 337 / 541 / rest and 127 / 359 / 563 / rest samples at 48 kHz, x 0.1..1 by `Diff Size`, faded in with the amount; gain 0.7 x amount). Each repeat passes them once more, so the echoes wash out repeat by repeat while the first echo stays clean, and the level is kept (|allpass| = 1). The chain delays the return by `dTot` (integer, the sides sum exactly), so the return is written `dTot` earlier in the tank and `tmin` grows by `dTot`: echoes stay on n * T. With no length (Diffusion 0) the return skips the chain, an exact bypass. Freeze's self-loop never passes it. Audio cases `diffuse_ref` / `diffuse` (echo 1 identical, echo 2 peak >= 3 dB lower, energy within 1.5 dB; measured -7.3 dB / -0.04 dB) and `freeze_diffuse`.
- Live custom unit formats (`parameter_unitstyle 9`, `parameter_units`): a literal `%` is written as a single `%` after the number (`Size %.0f %` -> `Size 60 %`); `%%` showed only `%` in Live.
- End: Ducking (threshold / release on the dry level, up to -24 dB) and Output gain on the wet, read from `hdr[40..42]`. Dry/Wet is equal power (wet = sin(90 deg x Dry/Wet), dry = cos), so the wet rises up to 100 %; the earlier Echo-style law (both at unity up to 50 %) stopped changing the wet above 50 % (audio case `drywet`).
- **Freeze** is a self-loop in the tank: while frozen the head writes an exact copy of the tank one loop period earlier (whole samples, latched per side when Freeze engages from the tap time in use without LFO / wobble; Ping Pong copies across sides). New input and the return from the chain fade out over 10 ms (`ig`) as the copy fades in, and the Feedback Clip is skipped, so what is in the buffer repeats at the same level whatever the chain or the Feedback knob does (the filter and the chain still shape what you hear, once). Before this, Freeze kept the chain in the loop at 100 % feedback, and any gain in the chain compounded (measured: +9 dB over 1.7 s with a 1.5x chain gain). Audio cases `freeze`, `freeze_gain`, `freeze_mod` require < 0.3 dB drift over 8 repeats.
- The tank write soft-limits where input and feedback meet (linear below 1, tanh knee to 1.8), inlined in the main body: the same expression as a GenExpr helper function that declares a local variable made the whole Start gen fail to compile in Max 9.1 (identity helpers compiled).

### Load time and idle cost
- **Deferred DSP (Start only, AGENTS 1d)**: measured in the VM, the Start gen's compile was about 0.5–0.9 s of the device's load, the End's about 0.1 s, all nine v8ui faces about 0.05 s. So `Delay+.amxd` ships without its `gen~`: the Start face embeds the gen patch (`GEN_DSP_TEXT`, `GEN_DSP_HASH`, built by `gendsp_json()` in `scripts/build_delayplus.py`) and `src/delayplus_dsp.js` creates `gen~ @gen /tmp/delayplus_start_<hash>.gendsp` one Task tick after `init_link` (live.thisdevice), wired from the `dp_genin` hub (`gate 1 1`; every cord that went into the gen goes to its data inlet, `to_gen_hub()`), `dp_plugin` and to `dp_plugout`. Until then `plugin~` goes straight to `plugout~` (dry keeps sounding through the chain); those two cords are removed once the gen is wired. The face re-sends the buffer binding and every gen parameter from the values it holds (`dpRaw`, `DP_GEN_PARAMS` = key -> [gen message, /100]) every 150 ms until scope `FRAMES` moves, then twice more; no `live.*` is banged (a bang flips a toggle `live.text`). The header shows `DSP LOADING…` / `DSP FAILED: …` in place of the link pill. Load trace: `Global("delayplus_trace").text` (`dsp ready after <ms> ms`); `scripts/test_delayplus_live.js` waits for it. The End keeps its static gen. The harness builds the gens directly, so the deferred path is only exercised in Live.
- **DSP**: the whole pair with every feature on is about 1 % of one core at 48 kHz in the VM (NRT render time, at the edge of what that method resolves). The filter coefficients (`tan` / `pow`) are recomputed only when a cutoff, the LFO filter modulation or the rate changes (`cHp` / `cLp` / `cFmL` / `cFmR` / `cSr`); output is unchanged.
- **Displays**: the page tab also sends `set_page` to `obj-viz`, `obj-lfodisp` and `obj-filtdisp`. The ring reads its 1536-sample history and redraws only on the Echo page, and sleeps after silence longer than the loop (its last bars have scrolled out) until signal or the header state (link, latency, MIN, freeze) changes. The LFO dots update only on the Modulation page, the spectrogram FFTs only while the Filter Display is open on the Echo page.

### Cross-device transfer (`src/delayplus_start.genexpr`, `src/delayplus_end.genexpr`)
- **Buffer names use M4L's `---` prefix** (`buffer~ ---dpxfer`, also in the message boxes). In Live, `#0` is NOT expanded in a device's top-level patcher (only in abstractions): with `#0-` names every Delay+ instance shared one header and transfer buffer, which looked like random timing jitter with more than one pair.
- Shared buffers live in the Start: `buffer~ ---dpxfer @samps 65536 @chans 5` (ch0/1 dry at Start index, ch2/3 return at End index, ch4 whitened mono tap) and `buffer~ ---dphdr @samps 64` (header; layout in the Start genexpr comment). The End's gen binds to them by name (`xfer <name>`, `hdr <name>`) from its link layer; until then it uses its own empty `---dpxferE` / `---dphdrE`.
- Each device runs a free counter (mod 2^22) and pokes it to the header. In Live, devices of one chain run in 32-sample vectors in chain order. End: K (End's sample `cE` carries Start's sample `cE + K`) = held min of `cS_latest - cE` (8 windows), **then calibrated from the audio**: every 8192 samples End cross-correlates its whitened input against Start's whitened tap (xfer ch4) over 128 candidate K around that anchor and adopts an unambiguous peak seen twice (`hdr[18]` = calibrated). Start: `Smax` = held max of `cS - cE_latest`; it reads the return at End index `s - Smax` (always written) and `B = Smax - K` (hdr[3]).
- **Latency compensation**: End reads dry at Start index `cE + K - L` (aligned with the wet; Live's PDC then delays the track by L). Start adds the return into the tank at `s - B - L` (the tap's original emit position), so the tap re-reads it exactly T later: echoes land on n * T for any chain latency. Requires `T > B + L + mod + 4` -> clamped, `MIN x ms` shown in the header (`scope[2]`, `scope[3]`). Any change of L / B / K ducks the loop for 2048 samples.
- `L` = sum of `latency_in_samples` of the devices strictly between the pair (a rack counts as its longest chain, recursively: Live reports 0 as a rack's own `latency_in_samples` even with a Limiter inside, `dpDeviceLatency` / `dpLatencyNode`), polled by the Start's link layer every 500 ms and **poked into `hdr[30]`** (the delay time in ms goes to `hdr[31]`). Messages sent to gen from the link layer's Task stopped arriving in Live, so values are written to the header buffer directly; the `lat` / `tms` gen Params remain for the standalone harness.

### Link layer (`src/delayplus_link.js`, shared by Start and End v8ui)
- Registry `Global("delayplus_bus").reg[<LOM device id>] = {role, xfer, hdr, t, polls, sentLat, lats, status}`, refreshed every 500 ms on each instance's own Task (stale after 3 s). Pairing = bracket matching over the own chain's device list (`dpFindPartner` in `src/delayplus_config.js`), so nested pairs and renamed devices work. All LiveAPI calls run on the instance's own Task, never in another instance's callback.
- Tempo sync: the Start observes `live_set tempo` and sends `tms` (ms) to gen (`dpTargetMs`).

### Chain latency comes from the LOM only
- There is no debug ping any more: `L` is always the LOM sum above. The impulse-based check (Debug parameter, `hdr[8..11]`, `hdr[17]`) was removed; header slots 8..11 and 17, Start scope 7 / 23 and End scope 3 / 9 are unused. Timing is verified by the audio harness (echoes on n * T for L 0 / 256 / 1000) and the Live test (`L == LOM` while a Limiter's lookahead changes).

### Build / Test / Preview
```bash
npm run -s typecheck && python3 scripts/build_delayplus.py && node tests/test_delayplus.js && for v in "Delay+" "Delay+ End"; do python3 scripts/render_preview.py "devices/$v.amxd"; done
```
- `tests/test_delayplus.js`: config math, pairing, a sample-level reference simulation of the slice-based two-device loop (echoes exactly on n * T for several L / slice sizes), and structural checks of the built devices.
- Audio checks (real Max render, Start gen -> `delay~ L` -> End gen in one patch, shared `scripts/max_harness.py`): `scripts/vm_delayplus.sh audio` (runs `python3 scripts/run_delayplus_audio_tests.py` in the VM).
- **In Live (tart VM, see 1a)**: recommended `scripts/vm_delayplus.sh run` (live + test under the Live lock). `delayplus_test.als` (generated by `scripts/make_delayplus_sets.py`: tracks `Delay+ 1/2/3` with rack `[Delay+, Limiter 6 ms, Delay+ End]`, MCP Bridge on Main) references the devices by absolute path, so it is rebuilt inside the VM. `scripts/vm_delayplus.sh live` syncs the repo to `~/ableton` in the VM, rebuilds there and (re)starts the VM's Live with that set (a rebuilt `.amxd` only loads when the set is reopened; Live is the only instance in the VM, so there is no "Another instance" dialog). `scripts/vm_delayplus.sh test` then runs `scripts/test_delayplus_live.js` on the host through an ssh tunnel (local 19877 -> VM bridge 9877): link, K/B, `L == LOM sum`, K calibrated from a Core Library drum loop it creates and plays, and the L in use following the LOM for Limiter lookaheads 1.5 / 3 / 6 ms (64 / 128 / 256 samples at 44.1 kHz), also with the Limiter inside an Audio Effect Rack (the test builds the rack through the LOM and puts the plain Limiter back). `scripts/vm_delayplus.sh audio` runs the audio harness in the VM.


---

## 16. Shared Cepstrum Engine (`src/cepstrum_lib.genexpr`) — Used by Glitch

- GenExpr helper functions prepended by each build script to its own `*_core.genexpr`. The header comment holds the full API and a core skeleton; `tests/cepstrum_lib_core.genexpr` is a runnable example and `src/cepstrum_ref.js` is the Node reference mirror used by every device unit test.
- **Data is passed by reference**: functions take `Data` arguments, but a function body cannot see a global `Data`, so the core declares `tab(4096,3)`, `win(4096)`, `fx(4096,2)`, `fc(4096,2)` plus per-channel `ring(4096)`, `acc(4096)`, `st(4)` and passes them in.
- API: `fft_init`, `fft_run(x,tab,n,inv)`, `stft_win`, `stft_in` (returns 1 at a hop, hop = N/4), `stft_frame`, `stft_ola`, `stft_out`, `cep_from_spec`, `cep_fold_minphase`, `cep_fold_maxphase`, `cep_to_logmag`, `cep_exp_spec`, `cep_remag`, `cep_peak`, `cep_peak_conf`, `cep_peak_refine`. N is a runtime power of two (512..4096).
- **Latency**: the STFT path is `N - 1` samples. Live's device latency is the static patcher attribute `"latency"` (no runtime message exists), so every device with a selectable `Size` pads the wet path with a `Delay` to a constant **4096** samples, delays dry identically, and sets `"latency": 4096`.
- Tests: `node tests/test_cepstrum_lib.js`; real Max: `python3 scripts/run_cepstrum_lib_audio_tests.py`.
- **GenExpr gotchas learned here** (all cost hours):
  - Never name identifiers `phi`, `pi` or `e<digits>` — they are built-in constants (a `phi` upper bound once silently shrank a pitch-peak search to one bin).
  - A variable first assigned inside an `if` block is not visible after it; use top-level temps or `x = cond ? a : x`. Values computed at hop time are safest written to a `Data` slot and read with `peek` at top level.
  - `wrap()` with a non-constant upper bound fails codegen with `use of undeclared identifier 'v_NN'`; use explicit arithmetic.
  - `z = fn(d);` with `z` unused is dead-code-eliminated — call work functions as bare statements.
  - A `History` written this sample reads back its old value.

## 19. Architecture of `devices/Glitch.amxd`

Morphing multi-FX glitch box: a pool of hidden FX modules in ONE fixed chain. **Randomize** rolls a new glitch (a subset of modules with random settings, shaped by **Chaos** and the Options allow-list), **Mutate** nudges it, the user saves glitches into **8 slots** and plays them back by **Mode**: Working (the glitch being edited), Morph (XY pad blending 4 corner slots) or Seq (16-step slot sequencer with glide, transport-locked).

- **Chain** (fixed order, so morphs stay smooth; cheap modules repeat, only one FFT stage; 23 instances): `drvA > filt > form > comb > phas > crush > rect > fm > corr > ring > stut > time > gpit > gstr > rvs > shuf > chop > SPECTRAL (one STFT, N = 1024: freeze, smear, cepstral crush, cepstral scramble, scatter, mirror, shift, gate/lofi) > drvB > dly > slap > rev > wid > soft limiter > Mix > Output`. Latency is always **1023** samples (patcher `"latency"`); dry is delayed identically. Modules with smoothed wet <= 0.0005 are skipped; the STFT runs only while the spectral wet and one sub-op are on. `gpit` / `gstr` share one `gl_grain` call site (loop of 2). Neutral is an exact identity.
- **Data model / contract**: header of `src/glitch_config.js` (module registry, flat param vector (113 module params with `wet` first per module, plus movement), glitch / state schemas (state v3 adds `seq`, v4 adds movement; older states migrate), `randomizeGlitch`, `mutateGlitch`, `glitchToVector`, `blendVectors`, `xyWeights`, `cornerSlotWeights`, `packEngineBuffer`, `randomizeSequence`, `seqWeights`, `seqSlotAt`, `filledSlots`, `glitchEngineConstants`, `glitchFaceLayout`). Morph: continuous params interpolate (log where the curve is log), wet interpolates, discrete params come from the highest-weight slot with that module active, an inactive module holds the active slots' values. Cepstral Crush / Scramble mirror `src/cepcrush_ref.js` (ported from the retired Quefrency Crush).
- **Engine buffers**: `#0-glitchslots` (1280 floats: 8 slot vectors of 113, 8 weights at 904, sequencer block at 912 = `[mode 0 Working / 1 Morph / 2 Seq, length, rate 0..9, glide, curve, 0, 0, 0]` + 16 steps with `-1` = hold, and a **constants block at 936** (param kind + 4 * group, defaults, wet index per chain position) that the UI and every harness must write: gen has no init block, because 565 init pokes cost about 3 s of compile). Gen re-blends every 32 samples with per-param smoothing. `#0-glitchscope` (`GL_SCOPE`: per-module activity / level, in / out 64-band spectra, cepstral bars, waveform rings, stutter / chopper state, `SEQ_*` step / slot / glide, sounding weights, frame counter). Layout tokens are substituted by `scripts/glitch_common.py`.
- **Sequencer**: gen computes the slot weights from the host beat (`gl_seq`, JS twin `seqWeights`); a step change forces a control tick so the switch starts on the exact sample; Param `playing` (`plugsync~` outlet 0) holds the clock while stopped; an empty sequence gives weights 0 (neutral).
- **Live params** (off-face `live.*`, driven through `route -> object -> prepend set_<key>` with `expectEcho` / `isStaleEcho`): Morph X / Y, Chaos, Mix, Out, Randomize / Mutate (edge triggers), Mode (3-way), Seq Rate, Glide, Seq Length.
- **UI**: one v8ui over the whole device (`src/glitch_ui.js`). Sections left to right: core | EDIT | SLOTS | SEQ | side; width `374 + 194 EDIT + 88 SLOTS + 216 SEQ` (374..872, `glitchFaceLayout(ui)`).
  - Core: morph pad, Glitch view, action bar (Randomize, Mutate, Chaos, Options, 3-way Mode). The pad and view show the **sounding** blend (`soundingWeights`: Morph = XY corners, Seq = telemetry weights). Glitch view tiles are audio-reactive glyphs with a chain badge: up to 4 big, 5–8 compact (4 x 2), 9–12 small (4 x 3), more than 12 page by 12, plus a 23-tick chain ribbon and pager. Clicking a tile opens EDIT (params, wet, lock-from-randomize, remove). Editing a module in Morph / Seq copies the sounding blend to Working and switches to Working (`beginWorkingEdit`, one undo step).
  - SLOTS: click recalls, shift-click saves, alt-click clears (or Save / Clear buttons); drag a slot onto a pad corner (or click a corner badge) to assign it.
  - SEQ: length flag, 16 steps (click cycles hold -> filled slots, shift back, alt hold; click a step then a slot pad / palette chip to assign and advance; drag slots onto steps), glide lane, Rate / Glide / Length bars, Eq Power / Linear curve, Randomize Sequence, Clear, PLAY chip.
  - Options (replaces the core): master / detail, 7 categories (toggle + select) and the types of the selected one; the allow-list used by Randomize and Mutate.
  - State blob `GlitchStateData` (versioned). Undo once per gesture.
- **Movement (phase 3)**: every chain instance has one optional movement source (`module.move`): Off, LFO (7 shapes: sine, triangle, saw up, saw down, square, smooth random, S&H), Steps (8 / 16 values with glide) or Env follower (attack / release on the module's input level). Params: target (one continuous param; the registry marks each module's main param as default), bipolar depth (on the param's own curve, so log params move in log space), 16 synced divisions or a free Hz rate, phase, step values. Gen evaluates all sources in the 32-sample tick through one `gl_move` call site, before the per-param smoothing (square edges smooth to about 3 ms). Synced sources lock to the host beat and hold while the transport is stopped; free ones keep running. A synced rate change adds a phase offset so the phase never jumps (a render started after a mid-run rate change can differ). Blend: depth fades with the weights (a slot without movement counts as 0), rates blend in log space, step values linearly; type, target and shape come from the heaviest slot that has movement. Randomize / Mutate assign and perturb movement (more at higher Chaos); lock-from-randomize covers it; Options has a Movement category.
- **Phase 3 layout numbers**: state **v4** (v3 migrates with no movement). Param vector 780 floats (113 module params, then 29 movement floats per instance at `mvBase + 29 * k`). Engine buffer `#0-glitchslots` 9472 floats with a **pack version at `SEQ + 5`**: gen re-reads slot vectors and constants only when the version moves, so a harness that writes in pieces must bump it last. Scope 640 floats (adds `MOVE_ON / OFS / POS / PH`). Gen compile about 3.6 s over a trivial gen; Live DSP ready about 4.4 s after script load.
- **Movement UI**: PARAMS | MOTION tab in the editor (`ui.edTab`, `glitchMotionLayout`): type selector, target, Sync / Rate, shape, depth, phase, step painter; a live band and marker on the target's param bar; a magenta motion pill on Glitch-view tiles. Test hook `test_move`.
- **Phase 4**: the chain is 25 instances: `... stut > ratc > time > scrb > gpit ...`. State **v5** (v4 loads with all new modules / sub-ops absent and their allow-list kinds on). Param vector 885 floats (258 module params + 25 x 29 movement), engine buffer 10752 floats (constants end at 10677), `mv_target` max 63. Scope adds `RAT_ON / POS / IDX / N / LEN`, `SCR_POS / SPEED`.
  - **Ratchet / Roll** (`gl_ratchet`): on a host-locked span (division, hash chance) the audio before the span is replayed as N repeats of geometric length `d0 * q^i` with `q^(N-1) = 2^(-3 accel)`, filling the span; pitch ramp over the roll, decay per repeat, 24-sample fades. Params: span, repeats, accel, pitch ramp, decay, chance.
  - **Scratch / Scrub** (`gl_scratch`): `lag = 4 + A (1 + p(phase))`, `A = depth * (buffer - 8) / 2`, `p` = sine / asymmetric triangle / smooth random on the host beat; a ~2 ms one-pole on the lag keeps turnarounds click-free; depth 0 = a 4-sample delay.
  - **Spectral order** inside the one STFT: freeze / smear, scatter / mirror / shift, `gl_mapfx` (Glitch Freeze, Stretch, Bubbler), `gl_magfx` (MP3ify, Duststorm, Thinner, Decapitate, Noisify, Clipper), Spectral Gate, Cepstral Crush / Scramble, `gl_phasefx` (Phase Swap, Robot / Whisper). Each helper has one call site (loops of 3 / 6 / 2). Params are read from the smoothed vector `pv`; host-locked seed = hop index of the host timeline (+77 on R). SpecOps (Unfiltered Audio) inspired the set.
  - **Spectral details**: MP3ify = 21 Bark-like bands, 1.5 dB scale factors, `x^0.75` quantisation to `1 + 31 t^1.5` levels (`t = log(kbps / 16) / log 20`), masking drop, HF cut `2.5k * 10^t`. Duststorm decodes with offset or permuted (`(b * a + s) mod 21`) scale factors re-rolled per host division. Glitch Freeze rotates held bins by `i^(k * frames)`; Stretch by `i^((k - sn) * hop)` (bin-accurate, 1–3 % off). Robot / Whisper level match `kb * (2 / kb)^(random^1.3)`, kb 1.5 / 1 (Robot on noise reads about -5.8 dB because its pulses hit the master soft limiter). Phase Swap x 1.6.
  - **Randomize / Mutate** cap spectral sub-ops at `round(1 + 3 * chaos)` (1 at Chaos 0 up to 4 at Chaos 100). Options: Spectral has 19 types, paged 8 per page (`ui.optPage`, `glitchOptionPage`); Time holds exactly 8.
  - **Numbers**: gen compile about 5.0 s over a trivial gen (the limit; the next additions must restructure, e.g. more ops behind one dispatch site), Live DSP ready about 5.7 s after script load. CPU (VM avg / max): all 19 spectral ops 7.2 / 12 %, Ratchet + Scratch 2.4 / 5 %, all older modules 7.0 / 12 %.
  - **Harness gotcha**: the harness result file truncates around 32 KB; let the probe reduce data (e.g. `dips:N`) instead of dumping large blocks.
- **VM directory**: `scripts/vm_glitch.sh` syncs to `~/Software/ableton-glitch` by default (`GLITCH_VM_DIR` overrides; the env does not persist between shells, so pass it inline on every call). Never point it at another device's directory: `sync` is an `rsync --delete`.
- **Preview caveat**: `render_preview.py`'s mock `text_measure` estimates width as `0.56 * fontsize` per character, narrower than the rendered font, so a tile title can appear to touch the motion pill in a PNG while `fit()` truncates it correctly in Live.
- **Deferred gen (1d)**: gen compile is about 3.1 s over a trivial gen, so the gen is created after load (`gate 1 1` hub `gl_genin`, `selector~ 2 1` passes dry until `dsp_on`, `dsp_ready` resends the binding, constants, Mix, Out, bpm, ppq). DSP ready 4–8 s after script load in the VM's Live (load-dependent); the GUI is up at once.
- **CPU** (VM, avg / max): idle 7.6 / 64 % (spike), 6 typical modules 6.2 / 11 %, all modules 9.6 / 19 %. The max figures are spiky under shared VM load.
- **GenExpr facts learned here** (Max 9): a scalar carried across loop iterations and passed as a call argument killed the compile (carry it in Data); a variable first assigned in a nested `if` is invisible after it; `round(peek(...))` can return wrong values (use `floor(x + 0.5)`, linted); constant-index Data peeks passed directly as call arguments can kill the whole compile (assign to locals); a nested Data peek used as an index returned wrong values (assign the inner peek to a local); a History read in the same sample gives the old value; a `gl_*` helper must be defined before use; a smear needs random phase for silent bins or it only attenuates.

### Build / Test / Preview
```bash
npm run -s typecheck && python3 scripts/build_glitch.py && node tests/test_glitch.js && python3 scripts/render_preview.py "devices/Glitch.amxd"
```
Extra previews: `python3 scripts/build_glitch.py --preview-editor|seq|options|many|many2|all`, then `render_preview.py tests/out/Glitch_<name>.amxd`. Audio checks (real Max, VM only, no retry wrapper): `python3 scripts/run_glitch_audio_tests.py [case ...]`. In Live (VM, under the Live lock): `scripts/vm_glitch.sh run` (DSP ready, Randomize via hook / parameter / face click, Morph X via the LOM, Seq mode steps with the transport, Seq Rate / Glide / Length reach gen, Mix 0 = dry). `GLITCH_VM_DIR` sets the VM checkout; use your own directory, other agents `rsync --delete` into theirs.

---

## 21. Architecture of `devices/Mega Modulator.amxd`

One Audio Effect that hosts up to **16 modulators** (LFO, Envelope follower, Random / S&H, Steps, MSEG) and maps each to any number of Live parameters, from a pool of **256 mapping slots** per device. Audio passes straight through (`plugin~` → `plugout~`, zero latency). The buffer names below drop their per-instance prefix, because the prefix is being changed (see the instance-isolation work).

### Files
- `src/megamod_config.js`: pure, Node-requirable helpers. It holds the constants, the state model and its (de)serialization, the cfg buffer layout, MSEG evaluation (taken from `src/mseg_editor.js`), the curve/transfer-table math (`mappingOutput`, `mappingTable`), JS reference modulators used by the tests, learn/target helpers and the self-modulation helpers (dest codes `selfDest*`, `classifySelfParam`, `intDescriptorFloats`, `selfCtlValue`). The **API CONTRACT** comment at its top is the UI-facing API.
- `src/megamod_mapper.js`: the v8 runtime (`MM`). It owns state, the engine buffer writers, pool slot allocation, learn, target resolution and validation, telemetry polling, `getvalueof` / `setvalueof` and width requests.
- `src/megamod_ui.js`: the UI: rack strips, plus the LST view (modulator inspector).
- `src/megamod_engine.genexpr`: the modulator engine (including the internal self-modulation matrix, see "Self-modulation").
- `scripts/megamod_common.py` + `scripts/build_megamod.py`: the builder. `scripts/megamod_layout.json` sets the initial width and the v8ui rect; it is kept at the LST view's width (588 px, 5 modulators) so the committed preview (`preview()` shows the LST view: Dice selected, its 4 mappings, Frequency selected) is not cropped. The device still resizes at runtime through `requestWidth`; `UB_PREVIEW_LIST = false` renders the strips view instead. `UB_PREVIEW_DMOD = 1 | 2 | 3` (with `UB_PREVIEW_DMOD_B` for the second view) renders a depth-modulation design with routes added; the committed value is `0` (a test guards it).
- Build order inside the v8ui: config, then mapper, then UI, concatenated into **one** script scope.

### UI (`obj-viz`, `v8ui`, `x = 4, y = 4`, width follows the device)
- **Rail (left, 20 px)**:
  - `LRN` learn button (the only way to learn): click = single-shot learn for the selected modulator (`MM.armLearn(sel, false)`, disarms after one mapping, pulses while armed); Shift-click = multi-learn (`armLearn(sel, true)`, solid, stays armed); click while armed disarms. Changing the selection retargets multi-learn to the new selection (single-shot stays on the modulator it was armed for).
  - `LST`: switches between the two views (the rack strips, and the inspector below). The state is `ui.pb`; turning it on with nothing selected selects the first modulator.
  - `OPT` (under LST, `ubRailRects().opt`, a vector cog glyph + label): toggles the **Options view** (`ui.opt`). OPT and LST are mutually exclusive (turning one on turns the other off, `ubToggleOpt` / `ubTogglePb`; a state with both flags is read as LST). With both off the strips view returns.
  - There are no tooltips and no TIP button (removed). Instructional hint text is gone for good (no Options hint lines, no footer ` | click a row to edit` tail, no "press LRN" empty-state text); hover state stays only for highlights and the list footer's target path. `settings.tooltips` is gone from the model; old saved states that carry it load fine (`sanitizeSettings` copies only the known keys, so no schema bump). Rail buttons (`ubRailRects()`: `learn`, `list`, `opt`) sit at `y = 3, 39, 75` (16 px, 36 px pitch), each label 27 px below its top, then the modulator count.
  - A modulator count `n / 16`.
- **Three views** (strips, LST, Options): the Options view is described below. LST off (and OPT off) = the rack strips; LST on = the modulator-focused **inspector** (`ubStrips()` / `ubListGeom()` follow `ubPb()`; the old all-modulators mapping list is gone, as are the experimental layouts). The width request follows the view: strips `22 + 58 per strip + 26`, LST `22 + tab column(s) + 204 + 6 + 172 + 6 + 144` (588 px with 5 modulators, 16 modulators use two tab columns).
- **Strips (56 px each)**: one per modulator.
  - The **upper-left corner glyph is the enable button**: type-coloured and lit when on, hollow grey when off. There is no LED.
  - Below it: name, live mini-visualization (full strip width, no output meter in the strips; the remap editor keeps its output bar), two key knobs and a mapping-count chip (the chip opens the LST view on that modulator). There is no per-strip map button.
  - Click selects a strip. Double-click expands it **in place** to a 204 px editor with the full controls for its type: the MSEG point editor, the step painter, LFO shape/rate/sync/phase, follower attack/release/gain, random rate/smooth. The type chip in the expanded header changes the type. The editor's chips, knob labels and the `N maps` chip all end at least 6 px above the card's bottom edge (`y = 143..155` for the chip).
  - Dragging a strip header reorders strips. The order is visual only and is kept in `state.ui.order`.
  - Double-clicking a strip's **header or name** expands it like a double-click anywhere else on a collapsed strip; on an already expanded strip a header double-click collapses it. There is no rename (names are not editable in the UI).
  - **Several strips can be expanded at once**: `state.ui.exp` is a list of slots (`ubExpList()`, `ubIsExp`, `ubSetExpanded(slot)` adds one, `ubSetExpanded(-1)` collapses all, `ubCollapseSlot(slot)` / `ubToggleExpanded(slot)` touch only one). A header double-click and the collapse chevron toggle only that strip, and the device width is the sum (`22 + 204 per expanded + 56 per other strip + gaps + 26`, via `ubLayoutWidth()` -> `MM.requestWidth`). Old states hold one number or `-1`; `ubExpRaw()` reads both (and drops slots that are not modulators), and the list form is written the next time the UI changes it. `ubExp()` is the most recently expanded slot or `-1` (kept for the test hooks). **Width limit (decided: refuse, no scrolling)**: the strips view has no horizontal scrolling, so `ubMaxExpanded()` = how many strips fit `MAX_WIDTH` (1600 px) with the current strip count; expanding one more is refused and a cyan HUD pill says "No room to expand: the device is at its maximum width" (`ubNote`). A saved list that does not fit (e.g. after adding modulators) is cut to the limit by `ubExpList()`, so nothing is drawn or given a face outside the device. Selection is separate: a click selects, expansion does not move it (expanding sets it to the strip, collapsing leaves it); the LST inspector shows the selected modulator and ignores the list, OPT too. Deleting an expanded modulator removes it from the list (`ubPruneExp`); undo expands it again (`ubDelWasExp`). Parameter faces are laid out for every strip of the view at once (`ubFacesWanted()`), one per pool object, hidden while a popup or popover is open (`tests/test_megamod_ui.js`: "multi faces").
  - A trailing `+` strip opens the type picker.
  - **Range editing from the strips (maps popover)**: the `N maps` chip (collapsed or expanded strip) opens a popover anchored to the strip; it edits the real mapping fields (Mod `range` with `polarity` unchanged; Remote `min` / `max`) in the mode colours (`UB_MOD_COL` cyan, `UB_REM_COL` orange). The LST view is reached from the popover's header `LST` button. `tests/test_megamod_ui.js` checks that every interactive rect of a strip is inside it and disjoint from the others (0 / 1 / 4 / 12 mappings).
    - **Layout**: roomy rows: `UB_POP_W` 260 px wide, `UB_POP1_ROW` 19 px rows with 8 pt names, 6 rows visible (`UB_POP_MAXH` 159) before the wheel scrolls (with one or two strips the device is widened to hold it while it is open, via `ubLayoutWidth()` + `ubSyncWidth()`). It sits at `x >= UB_RAIL + UB_GAP` (never over the rail), fully inside the layout width and the 161 px height, for the first, last and expanded strips. Header: modulator name and `LST` button. Row columns (same x in every row): **on/off toggle** (`pr.tog`, 11 x 10, hit kind `mp_tog`, the mapping list's power glyph `ubMapToggle` in the modulator colour; it sets `MM.updateMapping(id, {enabled})`, disabled rows dim, normal and self rows alike) | parameter name | `M` / `R` chip (click switches the mode) | range bar | **value text in its own right-aligned column**, sized per popover by `ubPopValW(maps)` to the widest text the rows' *modes* can show (all Mod: `±100%` / `+100%` / `−100%`; any Remote row: `100–100%`). The width depends on modes only, never on values, so it is constant while dragging and editing and changes only when a row switches Mod / Remote (all rows share it, so columns stay aligned). The bar takes all remaining width and ends 3 px before the column (`ubPopRowParts(R, y, vw)`, `P.vw`). **Self-mapping kind marker**: moved from the left badge to a tiny marker right after the name text (`ubSelfMarkSmall`: a 6 px green loop = INTERNAL, an orange `L` tag = VIA LIVE), so the toggle cell is the same in every row; the name is fitted to the column minus 8 px for it.
    - **Bars**: Mod bar = centred bar (drag sets `range`; `+` / `-` use one side, `±` both, a drag on the hidden side pins 0). Remote bar = two handles (the nearest to the press follows, only that field changes). Double-click resets (Mod range 1, nearest Remote handle to 0 / 1). Click outside or Esc closes (the click is swallowed).
  - **Delete and undo**: the Delete / Backspace key deletes the **selected modulator** with its mappings (`ubDeleteMod`; the X button uses it too); `Cmd-Z` / `Ctrl-Z` undoes, `Shift-Cmd-Z` / `Ctrl-Y` redoes. The stack lives in the mapper (`MM.undo()`, `MM.redo()`, `MM.undoInfo()`, 16 entries, a new delete clears redo, `MM.load` clears it). An entry is an exact snapshot: modulator slot and settings, each mapping (id, target, shape, mode, range, min, max) at its list index, its Live id, missing flag and pool slot. Undo re-inserts them and re-binds the pool through `syncMapping` (tables, routes and ids are rewritten); if the old slot was taken meanwhile the modulator returns to the next free slot and its mappings follow. Mapping deletion (the x in a list row) uses the same stack. There is no "Undo delete" pill (removed); the rail has no undo / redo buttons either: only the keys.
  - **Key input**: two paths into the same `ubKeyCommand`: the v8ui's native `onkeydown(keycode, textcharacter, down, cmd, shift, capslock, option, ctrl)` and a patcher fallback `[key]` -> `[pack 0 0]` (ascii + modifiers) -> `[prepend key]` -> `obj-viz` (`function key(ascii, mod)`, wired in `scripts/megamod_common.py`). Both act only while the UI is focused (`ubFocus`: set by any click or double-click on the UI and by `onfocus`, cleared by `onblur`), so typing elsewhere in Live does nothing; an echo of one stroke from the other path within 40 ms is ignored. There is no inline name editing (modulator names come from the defaults and the saved state; `MM.setName` stays in the API but the UI never calls it), so no text-entry state can swallow Delete. In the Options view Delete does nothing (the selected modulator is not visible). **Escape**: Max's native `onkeydown` reports Escape as keycode `-3` with textcharacter `27` (measured in Live); `ubKeyCommand` checks it first (it closes the type popup / maps popover). It used to fall into the Delete branch (`-3` was in the delete list) and deleted the selected modulator; `-3` is no longer a delete code. **Risk (needs a manual check in Live)**: Live may intercept Delete (it deletes the selected device) and Cmd-Z (its own undo) before the device sees them; if so the user must click the device first / use Live's own undo.
- **LST view = the inspector** (`x` from the rail, every panel `y = 0..161`, the same top and bottom edge, 6 px gaps between the editor, list and remap panel):
  - **Tab column** (24 px, a second column beyond 8 tabs): one tab per modulator (type glyph in its colour, a coloured left bar, the selected tab outlined and tinted; dimmed glyph when off), then a `+` tab (type picker). Click selects. Strips can't be reordered here.
  - **Expanded editor** (204 px) of the selected modulator: the same editor as the expanded strip, with the header `[enable glyph] name [type chip] [X] [<]`. The **`<` button returns to the rack strips** (the LST view's close; in the strips view the same button collapses the strip in place). The **X still deletes the modulator** (first click arms, second deletes; the selection moves to the next modulator and the view stays open). Double-clicking the fixed editor header does nothing.
  - **Mapping list** (172 px) of the **selected modulator only**: header row (colour dot / enable button, name, count; tinted `UB_TINT_HEAD`), then its mapping rows. The footer reads `<n> mapping(s) on <name> | click a row to edit` (`ubFooterText`: counts the shown mappings, shortens the name first, then the tail, so it always fits `lw - 14`); hovering a row shows track > device > param instead. The header count is the shown count too.
  - Rows: power-style on/off toggle at the left (`MM.updateMapping(id, {enabled})`; disabled rows dim), parameter name (hover shows track › device › param), a small icon of the mapping's own MSEG curve (click only selects the row), orange value (Mod: polarity glyph + range, e.g. `+50%`, `±50%`, `−50%`, drag edits the range; Remote: the window `20–80%` (en dash), drag shifts min and max together), M/R toggle, and × delete at the far right. Missing targets are red.
  - Clicking a row selects it for the **remap editor** beside the list: the editable **MSEG transfer curve** (x = modulator 0..1, y = output 0..1; click empty space = add a point and drag it, drag a point, drag a segment's centre square to bend it, double-click a point to delete it; the first and last points stay pinned to x = 0 / 1), 4 preset buttons (Linear / Exp / Log / S, each loads an equivalent point set; no Stepped preset here, `UB_PRESETS`) in a row at the bottom, and a top **tab row**: `MOD` / `REM` tabs (40 px each, the active one lit, cyan for MOD, orange for REM; same size and position in both modes) and, in Mod only, a segmented **polarity control `[+][±][−]`** at the right end (vector glyphs: plus, plus over minus, minus; 15 px cells, exactly one lit, sets `polarity`). There is no X / delete button in the editor (mappings are deleted with the × at the right end of the list row), no bottom MOD|REM toggle, no Amount bar and no MIN / MAX boxes: **the range triangles on the output bar are the only range controls**. The MSEG is 94 px high in both modes (the presets row ends at y = 159) and the editor sits in an orange frame in Remote. **Mod mode**: to the right of the MSEG a **centred output bar** (Phase Plant style, a bar instead of a dot): zero is the vertical centre, positive output fills up from it, negative down. One limit triangle sets `range` (its distance from the centre, the whole Mod depth): `+` shows only the upper triangle, `−` only the lower one (the zero marker is hidden, fixed at the centre and not a hit target; a press on the hidden side is ignored and a drag past the centre pins range to 0), `±` shows both triangles linked symmetrically (either one sets the range). Double-click = range 1. The band covers centre..limit (both sides in `±`); the fill is the live signed modulation `o`. With the modulator bypassed the editor and bar draw grey and the scan line / live value are hidden, but everything stays editable. It reuses the modulator MSEG editor's point model and gestures (`ubMsegTgt` / `ubMapTgt`).
  - **Remote editor** (`mode: "remote"`): no polarity control, no centred bar. Two **independent** limits `min` / `max` (absolute 0..1 of the target; min > max inverts). The Remote colour is the orange `UB_REM_COL`: the lit `REM` tab, the frame and tint around the tab row and MSEG, and the curve. The bar right of the MSEG is an **absolute 0..1 bar** (no centre line) with two independent triangles, max filled and min outlined; the nearest triangle wins when you grab, double-click resets that one to 0 / 1; the min..max band is shaded and the live fill runs from min to the live value. Each triangle sets only its own field. The detail panel has no mode branches left: one layout, built by `ubDetailRects` and painted by `ubPaintDetail`.
  - The list-row value drag in Remote shifts min and max together (width kept).
  - **Depth pill** (`ubDepthW()`, detail id `depth`, `ubPaintDepth`): a small slider-style pill `Depth 100%` at the right end of the name line of the remap editor (`y = 22..35` of the panel, 13 px tall inside its 15 px `live.numbox` face, right-aligned to the detail width, above the polarity cells, in both modes; the modulator name line shortens to leave room). The fill is the value. Horizontal drag (1 px = 2 %, Shift = 0.25 %), double-click = 100 %, wheel = 5 %; it edits `mapping.depth` through `MM.updateMapping` (so it also moves the Live parameter, see "Automatable parameter pool"). Popover rows have no depth control (no room that stays clean); the LST remap editor is the only UI for it.
  - The remap editor follows the selected modulator (its first mapping unless one of its rows is picked; a modulator without mappings shows `No mappings yet`) and sits on its own rounded panel (`UB_PANEL_W` 144 px, `ubListGeom().panel`, same fill / stroke / top / bottom as the list panel) with 6 px side padding; the range readout is plain text at the right end of the presets row (the presets shrink to leave room; its width is fixed to the widest possible text so the row never shuffles).
- **Options view** (`ui.opt`, `ubOptGeom()`, painted by `ubPaintOpt()`; no strips): one rounded panel (`UB_OPT_W` 360 px, `x = 22`, `y = 0..161`, the same fill / stroke as the list panel) with an `OPTIONS` header and four 35 px rows (`UB_OPT_ROW`, `y = 17..157`), each a label (no hint line) on the left and the control on the right. The width request is `22 + 360 + 8` = 390 px (`ubLayoutWidth()` -> `MM.requestWidth`).
  1. **Default mapping mode**: `MOD | REM` segmented control (48 x 14 tabs, painted with `ubPaintModeTab`, same style and colours as the remap editor tabs: Mod cyan `UB_MOD_COL`, Remote orange `UB_REM_COL`). Sets `settings.defaultMode`; new mappings made by learn or `MM.addMapping` start in that mode (existing ones are untouched). Mod default stays `±`, range 0.5; Remote default is min 0, max 1.
  2. **Global smoothing**: horizontal value bar, 0..500 ms, log feel (`smoothFromPos`: `ms = 500 * (101^p - 1) / 100`, half travel = 45 ms; below 0.25 ms it snaps to Off). Drag / click, double-click = Off, wheel. Text `Off` / `12 ms`. **Default 0 (Off)**, chosen so existing sets and the audio tests are unchanged.
  3. **Rate multiplier**: the strip knob painter (`ubKnobBody`, shared with `ubKnob`) with tick marks at the steps and the value as `×1.5`. Log scale 0.25x..4x (x1 at the centre). Drag (vertical, Shift = finer) is continuous in log space with a **magnetic snap** (`rateSnap`, +-0.045 octave) onto the musical steps `RATE_MUL_STEPS` = 1/4, 1/3, 1/2, 1, 3/2, 2, 3, 4, so there is a free fine range between the steps; the wheel jumps to the neighbouring step; double-click = x1. Text `×¼`, `×⅓`, `×½`, `×1`, `×1.5`, `×2`, ... or `×1.25` for a free value.
    4. **Self-mod**: `INTERNAL | VIA LIVE` segmented control (two 54 x 14 segments, `ubPaintModeTab`: INTERNAL green `UB_SELF_COL`, VIA LIVE orange `UB_REM_COL`), hint `Own controls modulated by the engine or Live`. Sets `settings.selfMod` (default `internal`) through `MM.setSelfMod`, which also converts existing self-mappings of the other kind (see "Self-modulation"). The rate dial shrank to `r = 9` to fit its 35 px row.
  Hit kinds: `rail_opt`, `opt_mod`, `opt_rem`, `opt_smooth`, `opt_rate`, `opt_self_int`, `opt_self_live`, `opt_panel`. Every rect is inside the panel and the 161 px height and none overlap (`tests/test_megamod_ui.js`).
- **Pool full**: a red pill "Mapping pool full (256)" shows when the mapper fires change kind `"full"`; with `id -1` (the internal matrix) it reads "Internal routes full (64)". A cyan note pill ("Converted N self-mappings (undo with Cmd-Z)", `ubNote`) follows a selector change that converted mappings.
- **Width**: the UI computes its layout width and calls `MM.requestWidth(w)`, which goes `width <w>` → `prepend setwidth` → `live.thisdevice`. `resizeFace()` grows the v8ui (looked up by its scripting name `MegaModState`, `MM_UI_VARNAME`) and every full-width background panel (varname = box id) with it.

### Engine (`gen.codebox~`, varname `megamod_engine`, hidden)
- **Slots**: 16 slots, all computed at audio rate. Per-slot config comes from `Buffer cfg("megamodcfg")` (16 × 24 floats). MSEG tables come from `megamodmseg` (16 × 1024) and steps from `megamodsteps` (16 × 32).
- **Inputs**: the mono sum (for followers) and transport from `plugsync~` (`hplay`, `hbpm`, `hppq` Params). `Param ratemul` (default 1, from the Options panel, sent as `gen ratemul <x>`) multiplies the rate of every LFO / Random / Steps / MSEG slot and does not touch the envelope follower. Free-running: the per-sample increment is `rate * ratemul / sr` (the accumulator is continuous, so a change never jumps). Synced: phase `= (beat - anchor) / div * ratemul + off`; when `ratemul` changes, a per-slot offset (`st[sb + 3]`, reset on restart) absorbs the difference so the phase stays continuous.
- **Outputs**: 16 signal outlets. Every generator yields `u` in 0..1. Bipolar output is `depth*(2u-1)`; unipolar output is `2*depth*u-1`, so the full −1..1 range always reaches the transfer table.
- **Telemetry**: `megamodscope` (640 samples). Slot `s` writes `[out, phase, u, aux]` at `4*s`. The header holds frame, sr, beat, bpm and playing at `64..68`. `128 + 12 * s + ...` holds the slot's effective (modulated) control values and `320 + k` the effective Depth of pool slot `k` (see "Self-modulation").
- **Config cache**: `cf` (the raw cfg, refreshed one slot per sample) and `cm` (the modulated copy the slots read; equal to `cf` unless an internal route modulates the field).

### Mapping pool (saved objects, no runtime scripting)
- **Why**: `live.modulate~` / `live.remote~` objects **created by JS scripting in a running device never attach to a parameter in Live**: no modulation ring, no value change. This was verified through the MCP bridge. Every mapping object must therefore be saved in the device file.
- **Routers**: the build generates **8 router `gen.codebox~`** boxes, each with 16 inlets (wired from the 16 engine outlets) and 32 outlets. Slot `k` = router `floor(k/32)`, outlet `k%32`.
  - Each slot reads `route[2k]` (source modulator index, or −1 when off) from `megamodroute` (1024 samples; see the Depth lines below).
  - It looks up its 256-sample region `k*256` of `mmcurve` (fixed at 256 × 256), with the same convention as `lookup~`: index `(x+1)/2 * 256`, linear interpolation, clamped to the last sample.
  - **Smoothing** (Options): `Param smooth` (ms, default 0) feeds a one-pole lowpass on every slot output, `ys = y + (ys - y) * smCoef` with `smCoef = exp(-1000 / (smooth * samplerate))`, 0 = bypass (coefficient 0, output = y). The mapper sends `smooth <ms>` out of the v8ui outlet 0 -> `route width gen smooth` outlet 2 -> all 8 routers' inlet 0. Chosen over the `smoothing` attribute of the 256 `live.*~` objects because it needs one message instead of 512 attribute writes, works at an exact time constant in ms regardless of the Live parameter, and also smooths the Remote path (`live.remote~` smoothing is a different, fixed behaviour). Cost: one multiply-add per slot per sample.
  - The whole mapping transform is baked into that table, so editing a range or the curve only rewrites 256 samples: `mappingOutput(m, x)` = evaluate `m.shape` (MSEG points) at `u = (x+1)/2` and apply the Mod polarity / range or the Remote min / max formulas (see State). Since schema 10 EVERY pool slot (all 8 routers, `0..255`) applies its Depth per sample: the table is baked at depth 1 and the router reads `route[2k+1]` (effective depth) and `route[768+k]` (Remote min or -1) (see "Depth-modulation routes"), so a Depth lane or a depth modulation is smooth and needs no table rewrite. (Before schema 10 only slots `0..63` did; 64+ baked the depth.)
- **Per-slot objects**:
  - `live.modulate~ @smoothing 1`, varname `mm_mod_<k>`.
  - `live.remote~ @normalized 1`, varname `mm_rem_<k>`.
  - Two `prepend id` boxes, `mm_mid_<k>` and `mm_rid_<k>`, wired to the **right inlet** of each. `id` must arrive on the right inlet; `Maxobj.message()` only reaches the left inlet.
  - The mapper binds the active mode's object and sends `id 0` to the other one. It always unbinds before binding, and on delete it unbinds both and sets the route to −1.
  - A new mapping takes the lowest free slot and **keeps it for life**: the slot is saved in the mapping (`mapping.slot`) and restored on load, so deleting a mapping never moves the others. Only a mapping with no valid, unclaimed saved slot falls back to the lowest free one. The 257th `addMapping` returns `null` and fires `"full"`.

### Automatable parameter pool (saved `live.*` objects: Live can only automate saved parameters)
- **Objects** (`scripts/megamod_common.py` `auto_boxes_and_lines`; not in presentation, `parameter_enable 1`, `parameter_invisible 0`, so Live lists them in the device parameter list, Map to Macro, MIDI map and Push): **96 modulator parameters** (16 slots x (5 generic controls + `On`); `live.dial` varname `ap_<slot*6+j>`, `j = 5` is a `live.text` Toggle `Off` / `On`), **96 switch parameters** (16 slots x 6, varname `at_<slot*6+t>`: `t = 0` is the LFO shape, a 5-value `live.tab` enum `Sine / Triangle / Saw Up / Saw Down / Square`; `t = 1..5` are `live.text` Toggles for `sync`, `bipolar`, `retrig`, `oneshot`, `loopOn`), **64 depth parameters** (`ad_<k>`, `live.numbox`, mapping pool slot `k < 64`) and one hidden marker `ap_marker` (`parameter_invisible 1`). Every object outputs through its own `prepend autop <i>` / `prepend autot <i>` / `prepend autod <k>` / `prepend autom` cord into `obj-viz`. The generic ones are Float `0..100`, `unitstyle 5` (percent); the marker is an Int `0..2` (nothing exceeds 255); all `parameter_linknames 0`, `parameter_initial_enable 0`. The device lists **257** parameters in Live (Device On + 96 + 96 + 64). `tests/test_megamod.js` checks the counts, unique varnames and long names, enable / invisible flags, not in presentation, types, ranges, cords and that the background panels stay last.
- **N = 5 generic controls** (the max over the types), assigned per type by `AUTO_KEYS` (`src/megamod_config.js`); Depth is always the third: LFO `rate, syncDiv, depth, phase, -`; Envelope `attack, release, depth, gain, -`; Random `rate, syncDiv, depth, smooth, seed`; Steps `rate, syncDiv, depth, steps, glide`; MSEG `rate, syncDiv, depth, loopStart, loopEnd`. Rate **and** Div are both there (the one `sync` selects is audible). **Switches** (`AUTO_SW_KEYS` = `shape, sync, bipolar, retrig, oneshot, loopOn`, `AUTO_SW_USED[type]` says which a type shows) have a FIXED meaning for every type, so they get real Live names: `M3 Shape`, `M3 Sync`, `M3 Bipolar`, `M3 Retrig`, `M3 1Shot`, `M3 Loop` (`autoSwitchName`); the value is the model value itself (`0/1`, shape `0..4`), not a percent. N stays 5: no type has more than 5 numeric controls (Random, Steps and MSEG have exactly 5 with Rate and Div both counted). Not automatable (not parameters): step values, MSEG points, and the buttons (type chip, delete, collapse, count chip).
- **Value curve** (`autoNorm` / `autoValue`): parameter = `norm * 100`, mapped with the same curve as the UI knob (log for `rate` / `attack` / `release`, linear otherwise, integer controls round); a test compares every `ubKnobs` spec with it. **Display**: Live shows percent of the control's travel (`str_for_value`: `50.0 %`), not Hz / ms / dB, because one generic parameter has a different range per type and a saved object's range cannot depend on the type.
- **Names are fixed: `M3 A` .. `M3 E`, `M3 On`, `M3 Shape` / `Sync` / `Bipolar` / `Retrig` / `1Shot` / `Loop` (slot 3) and `Depth 5` (pool slot 5)** (`autoSlotNames`, `autoSwitchName`, `autoDepthName`). **Runtime renaming does not work in Live 12.4.6** (verified in the VM through the bridge, `scripts/probe_megamod_rename_live.js`): `setattr("_parameter_longname", n)` (also with `_parameter_shortname`, `annotation_name`, without the underscore, as messages, followed by toggling `_parameter_invisible` / `_parameter_enable` / `parameter_mappable`) changes the Max object (`getattr` reads the new name back) but `DeviceParameter.name` / `original_name` stay what the file says, after 0.3 s, 3.3 s, `select_device`, device off / on and collapse / expand. So the meaning of a letter depends on the modulator type (`autoControlLabel(type, j)`, e.g. Envelope: A attack, B release, C depth, D gain) and the UI says it in the tooltips: the strip knobs and enable button end with ` | Live: M3 A`, the Depth pill with ` | Live: Depth 5` (or "no Live parameter (pool slot 65+)"). Renaming at device load (from the blob) is not done either: it would not follow a type change. `test_rename` (modes 0..11) and the probe script stay for re-checking a newer Live.
- **Sync rules (no feedback loops)**:
  - UI -> Live: `MM.setParam` / `MM.updateMapping({depth})` **message** the value into the object (`o.message("float", v)`, never `set`), so it outputs and Live records automation when armed (a type change or an undo pushes every control of the slot, switches included; the chips push their switch through `autoPushKey`). The echo (the object outputs it again into `autop` / `autod`) is recognised by value (`autoSent`) and ignored.
  - Live -> model (automation playback, macro, MIDI map, the parameter list, a click on a face): `autop(i, v)` / `autot(i, v)` / `autod(k, v)` -> `MM._autoParamIn` / `_autoSwitchIn` / `_autoDepthIn` write the model (a switch the type does not show, e.g. Envelope `Sync`, is ignored), the engine cfg (or the throttled mapping table) and fire a `param` / `mapping` change for the redraw. They **never push back, never `markDirty` / `notifyclients` (automation must not dirty the Set), never touch the undo stack**; a value that maps to the model's current value is a no-op (integer controls round, so a quantised value does not re-trigger anything).
  - Deleting a modulator leaves its parameter values; undo re-applies them from the snapshot. A released mapping slot's depth parameter goes back to 100 %.
- **Load order** (`autoInit`, when names + LOM are ready): nothing is pushed before `ready`; objects that output while Live restores them are only cached. At `ready`, **if the marker (`ap_marker`, saved with the Set) reads 1 or 2, Live's stored values win** (the switches only at marker 2: a Set saved before the switches existed has them at their initial 0, so its marker 1 keeps the blob's switch values, pushes them and upgrades the marker to 2): every parameter's value (from the restore output, else `getvalueof`) is adopted into the model and the engine (automation or a macro may have moved it; the blob can be stale), a missing value falls back to pushing the blob's. **If the marker is 0** (a new device, or a Set saved before this feature, which has no stored parameter values) **the blob's values are pushed** into the parameters once and the marker is set to 2. A `setvalueof` after ready (preset / Live undo) means **the blob wins** and the parameters follow (`autoPushAll(true)`). Verified in Live (`scripts/test_megamod_reload_live.js`, `duplicate_track` clones a device through its stored state like a Set load: with the model frozen at 70 % while the parameter was set to 30 %, the clone loaded 30 % and `init = {stored: true, marker: 1, cached: 71}`; a fresh device reported `stored: false`). Saving a Set through the UI could not be automated (the File > Save click wrote nothing), so a real save / reopen was not run. Mapping depth parameters belong to the pool slot, and each mapping keeps its slot across deletes and reloads (`mapping.slot`), so a lane on `Depth <k>` stays with its mapping; a new mapping may reuse a freed slot and inherit that slot's (now unbound) lane.
- **Limits and cost**: mappings in pool slots >= 64 keep a UI-editable depth but have no Live parameter. The pool (with the 96 switch parameters) is 1,575 boxes and 1,710 cords: `Mega Modulator.amxd` 961,259 bytes without the pool -> 1,199,351 (161 parameters) -> 1,601,610 bytes (257 parameters). Load in Live (VM, `scripts/measure_megamod_load.py`, 5 runs, set opened -> bridge sees the device's parameters -> script ready): no pool 8.7 / 8.7 s, 161 parameters 8.7 / 8.7 s, 257 parameters 8.6 / 8.6 s median (params visible / ready; bridge up 8.2 s): the difference is below the 0.25 s polling step, the load is dominated by Live and the gen compile.

### Learn
- **Observer**: `init_lom` (from `live.thisdevice`, never `loadbang`) observes `live_set view selected_parameter`. LiveAPI observers pass **one array** (`["selected_parameter", "id", N]` or `["id", N]`); flatten it before parsing.
- **Deferral**: the selection is handled on the next scheduler tick (`Task.schedule(0)`). Never read the LOM or touch objects inside a Live notification.
- **Lookup**: `describeParameter` uses a fresh `new LiveAPI(cb, "id N")`. Reusing a `goto` on a shared API returned nothing.
- **Rejected selections**: duplicates (same modulator + same target) and the device's own parameters that are not valid self-mod targets (`Device On`, `MegaModState`, the marker, anything of the source modulator itself) are ignored. A valid own parameter (a pool parameter of ANOTHER modulator, or any `Depth`) creates a self-mapping of the Options selector's kind (see "Self-modulation").
- **Persistence**: targets are stored by LOM path plus track/device/parameter names. On load they are resolved by path first, then by name among top-level devices. Unresolved mappings are kept and marked `missing`, and a 1.5 s poll revives them or flags them.

### Self-modulation (schema 9): a modulator modulating this device's own controls
One modulator can modulate any automatable parameter of the device itself (LFO 1 -> LFO 2's Rate, an Envelope -> a mapping's Depth, ...), in two interchangeable ways chosen by the Options selector `Self-mod: INTERNAL | VIA LIVE` (`settings.selfMod`, default `internal`; the selector only decides what NEW self-mappings are, see "Switching the selector"). Feedback between modulators (LFO 1 <-> LFO 2) is allowed.
- **Targets = every parameter of the pool**, each with a dest code 0..255 (`selfDestFor` / `selfDestInfo` / `selfDestName` / `selfDestFromName` in `src/megamod_config.js`; the code IS the Live parameter, so a learn only needs the parameter's name): `0..95` = `slot * 6 + j` (`j` 0..4 the generic controls `M<k> A..E`, `j` 5 = `M<k> On`; the same number as the `ap_<i>` object), `96..191` = `96 + slot * 6 + t` (`M<k> Shape / Sync / Bipolar / Retrig / 1Shot / Loop`, the `at_<i>` objects), `192..255` = `192 + k` (`Depth <k+1>`, the Depth of the mapping in pool slot `k`, the `ad_<k>` objects; since schema 10 an INTERNAL learn on one of these makes a depth route, see "Depth-modulation routes", and engine destinations `192..447` address the depth of any of the 256 pool slots). Not targets: the marker, `Device On`, `MegaModState`, step values and MSEG points (not parameters).
- **Learn gesture** (both kinds): arm LRN, then touch the knob (a click / drag on a drawn control's transparent face selects that Live parameter, so the `selected_parameter` observer sees the device's own parameter id). `_selectionChanged` no longer drops own parameters: `classifySelfParam(name, srcMod, state)` accepts a pool parameter and rejects the others, a **self-loop** (any control, switch or On of the SOURCE modulator itself: a modulator that switches itself off stops producing the signal that would switch it on again, and a control driven by its own output is a degenerate loop) and an empty modulator slot. Another modulator's controls, its `On`, every switch and any `Depth` are fine. Duplicates (same source + same dest code, in either kind) are ignored (`targetKey` = `self:<code>`). The mapping is made by `MM.addSelfMapping(mod, dest[, kind, info])` (kind default `settings.selfMod`); `addMapping` still refuses an own parameter (an internal target always goes through `addSelfMapping`).
- **INTERNAL (default)**: `target = {internal: true, selfDest, kind, slot, ctl, path: "", paramName: "M2 A", ...}` (`makeInternalTarget(code)`), Mod only (`sanitizeMapping` forces `mode: "mod"`; the REM tab / the list's M/R chip are inert and say "Mod only"), the usual polarity / range / depth / MSEG shape / enable. It uses **no pool slot, no live.* object, no Live id**: it lives in the engine's internal matrix (route `mapping.slot` 0..63, `MM.internalCapacity()`; the 65th fires change kind `"full"` with `id -1`, the UI pill reads "Internal routes full (64)", and no pool slot is consumed). Status: `ok` / `disabled` / `missing` (never `pending`).
  - **Buffers** (`megamodint` 512 floats, `megamodicurve` 64 x 256; `INT_*` in config, static-checked by `tests/test_megamod.js` against the genexpr): route `r` at `4r` = `[source + 1 (0 = off), dest code, 0, 0]`, `[256]` = highest used route + 1 (0 = the engine skips the whole matrix), `[264 + (type * 5 + j) * 5 + {0..4}]` = control descriptor `[CF field + 1, lo, hi, log, int]` per type / generic control (from `AUTO_KEYS` and `MOD_PARAM_RANGE`, so the engine has no second copy of the ranges), `[414 + t]` / `[420 + t]` = switch `t`'s CF field + 1 and max (shape 4, toggles 1). `megamodicurve[r * 256 ..]` is the route's transfer table, the same `mappingTable()` and `(x+1)/2 * 256` convention as the router, depth baked in (an internal mapping has no Depth parameter). `MM.flushInternal` writes everything on load / names / ready; a disabled mapping, a disabled source or a missing destination writes source 0 (the route is off, so a table read of a silent source cannot leave a stale offset).
  - **Engine** (`src/megamod_engine.genexpr`, step 3c, before the slots are evaluated): per sample, every active route reads its source's **previous-sample** output (`outv` still holds last sample's value when the matrix runs, so loops are stable with one sample of delay), maps it through its table (`o` in -1..1 = the Mod offset, i.e. `range * (...)` x depth) and the offsets of all routes into one destination are **summed** (`dsum` / `dcl`, one destination is applied once). The slots read `cm`, a modulated copy of the config cache `cf` (`cm` is refreshed together with `cf`, every 16 samples per slot, so a route that goes away restores the stored value within 16 samples); the matrix only overwrites the modulated fields, so the CPU cost with no routes is one `peek` of the route count per sample.
  - **Scaling per control (normalized knob space)**: a numeric control A..E is offset in its **normalized 0..1 knob space**, using the UI knob's own curve (`autoNorm` / `autoValue`: log for `rate` / `attack` / `release`, linear otherwise): `n = curve(stored)`, `n' = clip(n + o, 0, 1)`, `value = curve^-1(n')` (integer controls: Steps count, Div, Seed are rounded) and the result is used directly (clamped to the control's range by construction). One octave of an LFO's Rate is therefore `ln 2 / ln 4000` = 0.0836 of the knob (log 0.01..40 Hz) = a mapping range of 8.4 %: `range 0.0836` on LFO 2's Rate makes it swing between 1/2 x and 2 x. The rate multiplier (`ratemul`) is applied after, to the modulated rate; when the LFO is **synced** the Rate control (A) is not what runs it (the Div control B is: a mapping on B modulates the division index, on A it has no audible effect, and the UI draws the Rate knob's face as B). `selfCtlValue(type, j, base, off)` is the JS reference; the tests compare it with the descriptor-driven formula for every type, control, offset and base.
  - **Toggles, On and the Shape enum**: modulated by thresholding the modulated normalized value, per sample: a toggle / `On` is on while `clip(stored + o, 0, 1) > 0.5` (so a stored-off toggle with `+-` range 1 turns on while the source is above 0.5; a stored-on one turns off while it is below -0.5); `Shape` steps through its 5 values: `round(clip(shape / 4 + o, 0, 1) * 4)`. A mid-run `Sync` flip re-anchors the phase (no special handling), a `Retrig` / `1Shot` / `Loop` flip takes effect at the next cycle event.
  - **Depth targets** (extended to all 256 pool slots in schema 10, see "Depth-modulation routes"): every pool slot applies its Depth **in the router per sample** (all 8 routers, `router_gen_code`): the table is baked at depth 1 and the router computes Mod `y * d`, Remote `min + (y - min) * d` from `route[2k+1]` (the effective depth, written by the engine: base from `route[512 + k]` (mapper, = the mapping's depth) plus the summed internal depth offset, clipped 0..1; one slot per sample is also copied base -> effective, so a base change arrives within 256 samples = 5 ms) and `route[768 + k]` (Remote min, or -1 for Mod). The `route` buffer stays 1024 floats: `[0..511]` source / effective depth of 256 slots, `[512..767]` base depth, `[768..1023]` Remote min. The router sees the engine's value at the end of the audio vector (no per-sample alignment between two `gen~` objects), so a depth modulation is block-rate stepped (about 1.3 ms at 64 samples: fine for LFO rates, audible as zipper only for audio-rate depth modulation). Writing the depth is two floats (`writeDepthCells`), never a table.
  - **Telemetry** (`megamodscope`, 128 -> 512 -> 640 floats): `128 + 12 * slot + [0..4]` = the effective normalized A..E, `+ 5` the effective On, `+ 6..11` the effective switches (shape 0..4, toggles 0/1); `320 + k` = the effective Depth of pool slot `k` (`k` 0..255 since schema 10) (the engine's `cm` after modulation; `MM.readTelemetry()` -> `tel.eff[slot] = {ctl, on, sw}`, `tel.depth[k]`).
- **VIA LIVE**: a normal pool mapping (`mode` Mod or Remote, a pool slot, `live.modulate~` / `live.remote~`, `Depth <k>` lane and all) whose target is the device's own Live parameter, `target.selfDest = code` marks it (`ownTarget()` finds the parameter by name on `this_device`, `bindOwnPid`). Measured in Live 12.4.6 (VM, `scripts/test_megamod_selfmod_live.js`, evidence below): **modulating the device's own parameter works**: the pool's `live.modulate~` is bound to the device's own parameter id, the LOM `DeviceParameter.value` keeps the STORED value while the parameter's `live.dial` **outputs the modulated value** into `autop` / `autot` / `autod`, and the engine follows through that path. The rule that makes this safe (`viaDests`, `viaIn`, `rebuildViaDests` in the mapper): for a destination with an active **Mod** via-Live self-mapping, an incoming value goes to the engine's cfg float (or the router's base-depth cell) **only** - never to `mod.p` / `mapping.depth`, the undo stack, the dirty state or `autoCache`; the stored value is re-read from the LOM (`value`, 4 Hz `Task`, `viaReadStored`) so a real change of it (face drag, automation, macro) still reaches the model; a cfg rewrite of the slot re-applies the modulated value (`reapplyVia`); when the modulation stops (mapping deleted / disabled / converted / source off / type change) `restoreDest` writes the model's value back. A **Remote** via-Live mapping takes the parameter over (its value really moves, like automation), so it uses the normal path and the model follows it. VIA LIVE modulation updates at Live's control rate (not per sample) and costs a pool slot + two live objects; it is also visible in Live's own modulation UI and can be mapped / automated by Live, which INTERNAL cannot.
- **Rows**: INTERNAL rows carry a green loop glyph, VIA LIVE rows an orange `L` badge (list, maps popover, remap editor header: `internal route` / `via Live: M2 B`); the target is named by meaning, `LFO 2 Rate` / `Follow On` / `Wobble Depth` (for a Depth: the mapped parameter's name, `Depth 5` when the slot is empty; `MM.selfLabel(m)`, `selfDestLabel`), via Live with the parameter in brackets (`LFO 2 Rate (M2 A)`); in a narrow column the control alone is shown (`ubSelfFit`: `Rate`, `Shape`, `On`) and the hover text / footer spells everything out. **Rings**: a knob whose control is the destination of an ACTIVE self-mapping (`ubModKinds`, rebuilt each paint) draws a faint ring (`r + 3`) in the mapping's colour (internal green, via Live orange), a bright arc from the stored value to the live (modulated) value and a white dot at the latter; a modulated switch chip is outlined while its live state is on (with a corner dot), the `On` glyph and the remap editor's Depth pill (a tick at the live depth) get the same markers. Nothing is drawn for a disabled mapping / source or a missing control.
- **Switching the selector** (`MM.setSelfMod(kind[, convert = true])`, Options row 4): applies to NEW self-mappings, and **converts every existing self-mapping of the other kind** (the answer to "offer to convert": it always converts, as ONE undo step `{kind: "conv"}`, announced by a note pill "Converted N self-mappings (undo with Cmd-Z)"; Cmd-Z puts every mapping back exactly (targets, ranges, shapes, pool slots, Live ids) and Shift-Cmd-Z converts again). The selector itself is a setting, not part of the undo stack. Id, source, polarity, range, depth, shape and enable are kept. INTERNAL -> VIA LIVE: Mod stays Mod, the mapping takes a free pool slot and binds the own parameter. VIA LIVE -> INTERNAL: a **Remote** mapping becomes the closest Mod: an absolute window `min..max` becomes an offset of the same span in the same direction (`+` with range `max - min`, or `-` with range `min - max` for an inverted window; the offset is applied around the stored value, so it is the nearest equivalent, not an identical value curve). A conversion that finds no room (internal matrix full / pool full) leaves that mapping as it was and fires `"full"`.
- **Edge cases (decided)**: *Deleting a modulator* (UI X / Delete key / `removeModulator`) deletes the mappings it SOURCES and the self-mappings that TARGET it (`mappingsTouching`), as one undo entry; undo restores all of them in their old list order with their routes / pool slots, and if the old slot was taken meanwhile the modulator returns to the next free slot and the restored self-mappings that targeted it are re-aimed at it (`retargetSelf`). *Changing a slot's type* keeps its self-mappings and matches them **by position** (A..E, switch index): a mapping whose control exists in the new type keeps modulating whatever sits at that position (A is Rate for an LFO, Attack for an Envelope), the others become **missing** (red row, route off, no Live id bound) and revive automatically when the type changes back (`selfDestExists`; the Envelope has no E, Sync, Shape...). *Rate multiplier and sync* still apply (above). *Saving / loading* round-trips both kinds (`settings.selfMod`; the internal target is its dest code, the via-Live one a normal target plus `selfDest`; internal routes get their saved `mapping.slot` back, via-Live mappings their pool slot). *Caps*: 64 internal routes (the `"full"` event with `id -1`), 256 pool mappings. A modulator cannot target itself (see Learn), but it can target other modulators' Depth, On and switches.
- **GenExpr gotcha (Max 9)**: two helper functions (`normOf(raw, lo, hi, lg)` called with `peek(...)` expressions as arguments, `valOf(n, lo, hi, lg, it)` with five plain arguments) made the whole `gen.codebox~` silently produce zeros (every output 0, no error in the harness; the same `normOf` with plain variables was fine, so the trigger is not understood). The matrix therefore inlines its math; `tests/test_megamod.js` forbids those two function names. To bisect such a failure, the audio runner takes `MEGAMOD_GEN=<file>` (another engine file) and `scripts/vm_megamod_keys.sh audio [case ...]` runs it in the VM.

### Depth-modulation routes (schema 10): a modulator modulating the Depth of another mapping
Depth used to be modulatable only through a self-mapping onto the `Depth k` parameter (pool slots `0..63`, addressed by slot). Since schema 10 it is a first-class feature.
- **Model**: a depth route is an INTERNAL mapping whose target is `{internal: true, kind: "depth", mapId}` (`makeDepthTarget`, `isDepthRoute`, `depthRoutesOf` in `src/megamod_config.js`). It follows the target mapping **by id** across deletes, reloads and slot changes. Source = any modulator. It has the usual polarity / range / shape / depth / enabled, is Mod only (`sanitizeMapping` forces it, the REM tab and M/R chip are inert), and uses no pool slot, no `live.*` object and no Live id.
